## Codex r9q: REJECT — probe v2 canonical status REVOKED pending an empirical bound (2026-08-28 23:30 EDT)
The r9p recalibration is not defensible: (1) SCORE_TOLERANCE was already the changed-batching AGGREGATE bound; (2) 3.9e-3 is conditioner-TOKEN drift (context_audio, batch 1-vs-8), never mapped to cosine units, and the tie's actual regime (source 1-vs-16, cross-GPU) was never measured; (3) the sqrt(8) story is refuted by the data — the three near-envelope queries drift coherently (8/8 same-sign) with rederived AGGREGATE shifts up to 1.16e-3 > SCORE_TOLERANCE; (4) "separation" figures were dynamic range, not substitution evidence; (5) stale note + NPZ field residue. Reviewer confirms probe v2's ranks/calibration numbers are numerically intact — the tie is a validity gate, not a metric — but canonical status is withdrawn until the gate is honest. **r9r dispatched: measure the actual end-to-end per-sample cosine regeneration drift** (stratified candidate sample regenerated under the tie's batch shapes on BOTH GPUs vs frozen sidecars; plus a real substituted-observation movement distribution via cross-observation ties), derive the bound from the measurement, export typed constants, fix residue, then rerun the probe.

## r9r measurement (2026-08-29 ~00:30 EDT) + PLANNER RULING 3 — matched-batching tie
Measurement (both GPUs, 256 tie measurements + 16 whole-query matched replays = 92,616 waveforms; artifacts in `r9r_drift_measurement/`): (1) matched batching is **BIT-EXACT** (float32 aggregate delta 0.0, all 16 rooms incl. Cafe 5,295 cands); (2) changed-batching per-sample drift max 3.63e-3, aggregate up to 1.16e-3 (> SCORE_TOLERANCE on 4/256) — the r9p sqrt(8) story is dead, and the GPU axis contributes ZERO (identical digits both devices); (3) real substitution movement min 6.669e-3 (same-room nearby receiver), median 0.57. Separation 1.3x -> `derive_bound` REFUSED (as instructed). **RULING 3 (Planner): adopt the matched-batching tie** — regenerate each probe query's headline candidate at the run's own batch shapes through the engine's own path (measured bit-exact), tolerance = sidecar half-ulp only, separation vs closest adversary 27x, cost +~7 min/probe run. Option B (diagnostic-only tie) leaves the control permanently non-canonical; no other option dominates. Reversible; disclosed here for Yixun's veto. r9s dispatched to implement; probe v3 + consolidated Codex bundle review follow.

## 2026-11-02T23:52:38-05:00 — rB-F1 cycle 3: plan amendment A4 (i)–(iii) in code; the RESOLVED autocast in evidence (Codex rB findings 4 and 6, HIGH)
- **Goal** — A4 (i) "the G3 LOGIC gate is the production-partition-vs-faithful-replay comparison run with `--cond-autocast off` (fp32), keeping the hard per-key max |Δ| ≤ 1e-3"; (ii) "the same comparison under the resolved production autocast is run and PUBLISHED AS A DISCLOSED DRIFT MEASUREMENT with no pass/fail verdict"; (iii) "every gate's evidence records the resolved autocast state and dtype, never the word `default`".
- **Decision (brief: "your choice, documented")** — TWO flags, one engine per invocation: `--fa-production-parity-check` (G3, `--cond-autocast off`, 256/16, PASS/FAIL, `fa_production_parity.json`) and `--fa-production-drift-measurement` (G3D, `--cond-autocast default`, 256/16, `fa_production_drift.json`, `verdict: "measurement"`, `tolerance: null`, `match: null`, `complete` = the comparison's STRUCTURE held, `context = {vanilla_batching_caveat_max_abs_diff: 3.9e-3, batching_caveat}`; exit 0 iff complete). One invocation running both would need two model builds on one card.
- **Change** — `meshgrid_engine.cond_autocast_context` (the conditioner's context, now shared with `build_mesh_engine`) and `cond_autocast_state(device, mode)`: `eval_FLAC.resolve_cond_autocast` maps the flag, the context is ENTERED and torch is asked (`torch.is_autocast_enabled`, `torch.get_autocast_dtype`) → `{cond_autocast_requested, cond_autocast_enabled, cond_autocast_dtype, cond_autocast_device_type}` (default → float16 on CUDA, bfloat16 on CPU; off → float32); `build_mesh_engine` returns it in its context; `_gate_inputs` publishes it instead of the bare `cond_autocast`; `fp.GATE_AUTOCAST` holds G1 and G3 to off and G3D to the production flag in `finalize_evidence` (a mismatch is a FAIL / an incomplete measurement). `tensor_pair(..., tolerance=None)` and `_score_deltas(enforce=False)` give the measurement its no-threshold comparison.
- **Note** — `inputs.binding.cond_autocast` still carries the registered flag VALUE ("default"/"off"): it is the run binding verbatim (its digest depends on it); the execution state is the three resolved fields beside it.
- **Version Control** — commit `f382a582` (+108/−45 code lines, net +63; prose gate notes count as code).

## 2026-11-03T00:11:16-05:00 — rB-F1 cycle 6: the executed partition is CALL-derived, compared over the exact schema both ways; `assert_cacheable` checks the whole split (Codex rB finding 5 part 1, HIGH; NIT 8)
- **Goal** — Codex rB 5: "calculate batch/cap facts from observed calls, compare exact schemas symmetrically"; NIT 8: "validate complete split state in `assert_cacheable`, with malformed-split tests".
- **Change** — `FaPartitionRecorder.executed`: each `candidate_micro_batch` = the largest base batch OBSERVED, each `frame_avg_fwd_cap` = the largest rotated forward observed, `orbit_size` = passes counted (1 + Σ rotated rows / base), not the argument; the loop's configured stride is kept only as consistency violations (one stride per branch, no call above it, no rotated forward in the never-averaged `context_scored_batch`); `report().branches.<b>.{observed_max_base_batch, saturated}`. `fa_partition_differences` walks the union of keys at both levels (missing or extra on either side = a path). `assert_fa_partition_executed(final=…)`: before each row a TAIL-ONLY PREFIX (the loop at the declared stride, every call so far smaller) is consistent; the pass's final check, the merge and G3 are exact; the probe publishes what ran (`final=False`). `split_reasons` (engaged recorder, finite non-empty orbit starting at 0.0, no repeated angle) in `assert_cacheable`; `require_split` applies it.
- **Why the prefix rule (measured on the real audit, read-only)** — every z_band query of `Bathrooms/Bathrooms_idx_14` has 24–31 candidates (< the 32-candidate scored micro-batch; its unions are ≥ 29): a strict per-row check on call-derived values would refuse a BF shard whose first scored room is all tails, and the BF throughput probe on that room never fills a scored batch. Every other room has ≥ 33 candidates per query and ≥ 50 per union, so both registered shards saturate every branch.
- **Contract change (disclosed)** — `test_mixed_micro_batches_…`: a second chunking is now a VIOLATION ("ran at more than one configured micro-batch") instead of a moved headline (16 is still the largest observed).

## 2026-11-03T00:22:42-05:00 — rB-F1 cycle 7: the executed partition is DURABLE across resume and re-derived for every shard at the merge (Codex rB finding 5 part 2, HIGH)

## 2026-11-03T13:20:30-05:00 — rB-F2 cycle 2: the G3 / G3D evidence carries what its verdict was judged on (Codex rB-F1 finding 2, HIGH, part 1)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F1 2: "Include the pinned anchor digest/query digest in evidence and serialize the complete replay-partition record checked by `replay_partition_reasons`" (C5: the driver "discards `_anchor` before constructing G3/G3D evidence at localize_meshgrid.py:890"; "each G3 chunk publishes only `record["partition"]`").

Each chunk publishes the COMPLETE released replay record (`cond_method, cap_policy, frame_avg_fwd_cap, candidate_micro_batch, orbit_size, partition, n_orbit_forwards, angles_per_chunk, shared_angle_count`) and its per-key tensor / mask / DiT-input facts (shape, dtype, digest, finite, max |diff|, bitwise) — what the per-chunk clauses read; `executed_partition.counts` = the recorder's raw call counts (the executed block is re-derivable from the evidence).
- **Size** — the registered 16 probe queries have ≈ 11.6 k candidates (Cafe 5,295, Auditorium 3,722) → ≈ 370 scored chunks of 32 → the per-chunk facts add ≈ 1.3 MB to `fa_production_parity.json` (the committed off-grid probe report is 1.6 MB).
- **Contract change (disclosed)** — `chunk["replay_partition"]` is now the record dict (its `partition` is the old list); one assertion in `test_gate3_passes_on_a_batch_invariant_stack…` follows. The `_g3` fixture helper supplies a pinned anchor over its registered ids (`_anchor_for`).

## 2026-11-03T13:33:01-05:00 — rB-F2 cycle 3: `reverify_gate_evidence` — every G3 / G3D PASS clause recomputed from the serialized JSON alone (Codex rB-F1 finding 2, HIGH, part 2)
- **Goal** — the brief's HIGH 2 (c): "a pure function in `meshgrid_fa_parity.py` … that recomputes every PASS clause of G3 and G3D … FROM THE SERIALIZED JSON ONLY and returns named reasons".
- **Change, in two commits** — (i) `787b2efa` (refactor, behaviour-preserving): the gates now judge with predicates that read only published facts — `pair_reasons` (one shape, one dtype, finite, max |diff| ≤ tolerance), `mask_reasons` (presence, then the tensor clause), `score_reasons` (|dS| ≤ `SCORE_TOLERANCE`, argmax unmoved), plus `score_summary`, `chunk_summary`, `source_sizes`, `key_set_equal`; reason texts and order unchanged, except that a NaN |diff| / |dS| now fails its tolerance clause too (a non-finite value already was a reason); validated by the unchanged fa_split suite, 150 passed. (ii) `4b2af1f5`: `reverify_gate_evidence(evidence) -> list[str]` — `[]` iff the evidence re-verifies as the clean PASS it claims (G3D: as COMPLETE). Clause list for G3 / G3D: the registered production conditions (`batch_rows` 256, `source_chunk` 16, and `num_samples` / `prefixes` / `tau` / `seed` / `noise_policy` / `angles` / `micro_batch` equal to the published binding's) and `declared_partition` == the binding's `fa_partition`; the probe set (`probe_query_set` over registered vs the consumed entries, the published block and `inputs.queries` equal to it); the anchor (`anchor_reasons` + the recomputed key-set equality); per query the chunk tiling (starts / sizes at the micro-batch, candidate count); per chunk the complete replay record (`replay_partition_reasons`), the five ids' tensor and mask facts and the DiT inputs at the REGISTERED tolerance (structure only for G3D) and the chunk headline; per K the score clause (G3 only) and the published K set; the per-key fold, the digest chains, the coverage and the per-K summary re-derived from their parts; the executed partition re-derived from its raw call counts (`FaPartitionRecorder.from_counts`) and held to the declaration with no violation; the autocast clause; the identity re-derived from the published binding (`gate_identity`, every identity field compared); the provenance (`provenance_reasons`, its `complete` / `reasons`); finally the name, the REGISTERED tolerance, `score_tolerance` (1e-3 for G3, null for G3D), verdict / match / reasons (or measurement / null / complete) and `verdict_clauses` against the re-verified clauses. Tolerances, the pinned anchor and the production batching come from the code, never from the evidence; a malformed field is a named reason, never a crash. G3 / G3D notes name the function.
- **Tests** — real driver-run G3 and G3D evidence (registered 256 / 16 / K = 8 on the fixture) re-verify with `[]`, also after a JSON round trip; 18 single-field tampers of a real G3 evidence each yield their named reason (per-key delta above tolerance, per-key summary edited, a missing chunk, a wrong replay-partition field, an anchor digest mismatch, anchor ids not their digest, a missing probe query, a provenance clause flipped, a verdict clause flipped, score drift, argmax moved, executed counts edited, declared partition edited, binding moved, autocast edited, tolerance raised, coverage edited, reasons on a PASS); a G3D whose deltas are far above 1e-3 re-verifies (no tolerance) while a smuggled `score_tolerance` / `match`, an edited `complete`, a missing chunk or a moved pin are named.
- **Version Control** — `787b2efa` (+60/−40 code lines, net +20) and `4b2af1f5` (+154/−3, net +151).
- **Command / Validation** — RED 22 failed / 150 passed (`loc_meshgrid_2026-11-03_13:22:36_r2bf2_c3_reverify_g3_red.log`; no `reverify_gate_evidence`) → GREEN fa_split 172 passed.
- **Result** — passed.

## 2026-11-03T13:43:59-05:00 — rB-F2 cycle 4: `reverify_gate_evidence` covers G1, G2, GV and G0 too (Codex rB-F1 finding 2, HIGH, part 3)
- **Goal** — the brief's HIGH 2 (c): "(and of G1, G2, G0 and GV if the serialized fields already allow it cheaply — say which)".
- **Which** — G1, G2 and GV: their serialized fields already allowed it (G1 chunks carry full per-key facts and the released record; G2 its memoization facts and counter-test deltas; GV the vanilla report's per-id `equal` and the counter delta). G0: NOT as it stood — the frozen grid digests it compared lived only in the anchor file, and the published binding only as a digest — so G0 now also publishes each entry's `frozen_digests` and `inputs.published_binding` (the published binding's digested fields), two additive fields; with them G0 re-verifies fully. All six gates are now covered.
- **Clause lists** — **G1**: the chunks tile `n_candidates` at the micro-batch (start, size, `full`), the candidate list has `n` entries; per chunk the five ids' tensor + mask facts and the DiT inputs at the registered 0.0 (`pair_reasons` / `mask_reasons` / `inputs_reasons`) and the released replay record (`replay_partition_reasons`); the compared orbit == the binding's `frame_avg_angles`; coverage (full + tail) and the per-key fold re-derived. **G2**: the memoization's five ids, masks and DiT inputs at 0.0, its `match` re-derived; exactly the four registered counter-tests, each biting (> 0) on exactly its keys, `detected` re-derived; the orbit; the per-key fold. **GV**: the vanilla memoization `equal` on exactly the five vanilla ids, the counter delta > 0, `match` / `detected` / report `match` re-derived, the gate's query == the report's. **G0**: the probe set, the pinned anchor (`anchor_reasons`) and its key set (consumed == the anchor's ids, every entry with frozen digests), observation bank == expected == the frozen `ee2ba80a…` constant, the published binding hashing to `published_binding_sha256` and equal to the replay's binding on every non-exempt `RUN_BINDING_FIELDS` field, per query frozen == published digests (`frozen_digests_equal` re-derived), `float16_bit_exact` with 0 mismatches, aggregate |Δ| exactly 0.0. Plus, for every gate, the common autocast / identity / provenance / verdict / tolerance / `verdict_clauses` re-verification of cycle 3. Every finalized evidence now carries `reverify_note` naming the function.
- **Tests** — real driver-run PASS evidence of G1, G2, GV and G0 re-verify with `[]`; 21 single-field tampers across the four gates each yield their named reason (e.g. G1 a 1e-9 delta, a doubled replay cap, a deleted chunk, a DiT-input dtype, the identity's arm; G2 a mask delta, a counter that no longer bites, a deleted counter, a flipped `match`; GV a non-equal id, a zero counter delta, a moved arm or checkpoint; G0 a non-exact sidecar, a moved aggregate, a frozen digest, the observation bank, the published binding, the stated pin, a missing probe query, a lost hostname). One tamper expectation was corrected while going green: blanking GV's `expect_arm` is NOT a defect (GV's registered arms are P1 or YAW; `--expect-arm` is an extra refusal) — replaced by a moved arm and a moved checkpoint.

## 2026-11-03T14:06:10-05:00 — rB-F2 cycle 5: ONE validating ledger reader for every reopen and for the merge (Codex rB-F1 finding 3, HIGH)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F1 3: "Add one central ledger reader that validates every segment index, nonnegative count, per-segment digest, stored merged digest/executed/violations, and the full summary ledger block. Use it in both `open_fa_ledger` and `fa_partition_census`; add corruption-before-no-op-resume tests" (C3: "An edited count that preserves the headline partition can be re-legitimized by a no-op resume, which rewrites the aggregate digest and summary").
- **Change** — `meshgrid_engine.read_fa_ledger(out_dir, declared, exact=False)` is the only reader of `fa_partition_ledger.json`; callers: `open_fa_ledger` (every scored fa pass, at the top of `run_pass`, before anything is written), the driver's `preflight` (CPU-only, BEFORE the engine is built and before `claim_out_dir` writes the binding or opens a launch segment; a refusal is `REFUSED: out-dir …`) and `fa_partition_census` (`exact=True`, every shard, before the merge writes). **Checks** (all collected and named in one refusal): the ledger is valid JSON and an object; its `declared` is this declaration; the segment list is non-empty and its `segment_index` values are exactly 0..n−1 once each; per segment `n_queries` and every integer of every counts row (configured, cap, orbit size, count, each base and orbit size) is a non-negative int (bools refused), rows have exactly the recorder's seven fields, the counts are the canonical `to_counts()` serialization (no repeated / unsorted rows) and hash to the stored `counts_sha256`; the stored `merged` block (`counts_sha256`, `executed`, `violations`, nothing else) equals the recomputed sum of the segments; the run summary's `fa_partition_executed` block (`executed`, `violations`, `branches`, `note` and `ledger = {file, counts_sha256, n_segments}`) equals the recomputation over exactly ALL segments at a merge, and at a reopen is absent (a first invocation crashed) or equals the recomputation over exactly its first `n_segments` (an invocation finished, a later one crashed after adding a segment — the only legitimate stale state); a summary naming a ledger that is not on disk is refused; finally the summed partition is held to the declaration (exactly at a merge, as a consistent tail-only prefix at a reopen). A vanilla run (`declared` None) may carry neither a ledger nor a summary block (the merge's old rule, now also at reopen).
- **Tests** — on a finished fixture run plus a final no-op resume (two segments, as the driver leaves them), ten corruptions — tampered count, tampered segment digest, tampered merged block, missing segment index, duplicated segment index, negative count (with every digest recomputed so only the integer check can catch it), summary digest mismatch, summary block mismatch, a summary naming a deleted ledger, a garbled ledger — are each refused at reopen BEFORE a no-op resume writes anything (byte snapshot of the run directory unchanged, no conditioner call) and refused at the merge with nothing written; a stale summary (first segment only) is admitted at reopen and refused at the merge; the driver's preflight refuses a tampered count before any write, after admitting the honest run at the registered shapes.
- **Version Control** — commit `659e0d4b` (+116/−39 code lines, net +77).
- **Command / Validation** — RED 17 failed / 202 passed (`loc_meshgrid_2026-11-03_13:46:16_r2bf2_c5_ledger_reader_red.log`; three merge-side summary corruptions were already refused by the old merge checks — the gap was the reopen and the segment-level integrity; the garbled-ledger case added while going green is RED against the HEAD code, appended to the same log) → GREEN fa_split 221 passed; report + crossarm + engine + launch-record 465 passed / 2 skipped (the existing `ledger_counts_edited` merge refusal now names the segment digest and the merged block's `['counts_sha256', 'executed', 'violations']`).
- **Result** — passed.

## 2026-11-03T14:16:25-05:00 — rB-F2 cycle 6: a shard whose executed-partition check failed is NON-ADMISSIBLE, on disk and by name (Codex rB-F1 NIT 5; plan amendment A6 iv)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F1 5: "final partition failure leaves partial row artifacts despite 'publishes nothing' wording. Document these as incomplete, non-admissible artifacts, or stage/quarantine rows until the final exact check succeeds"; A6 (iv): "a shard whose final exact executed-partition check fails is NON-ADMISSIBLE: its rows are incomplete artifacts, refused by merge and report with a named reason, and the shard is restarted (never resumed) after the cause is fixed".
- **Change** — the wording: `FA_EXECUTION_NOTE` and the `assert_fa_partition_executed` refusal no longer say "publishes nothing"; they say the pass stops, its rows stay on disk as incomplete non-admissible artifacts, and the shard is marked. The marker: a scored fa pass whose FINAL exact check fails — and, by the same rule, whose before-row check fails (a disclosed superset: the same predicate family, the same incomplete rows) — writes `partition_check_failed.json` (`FA_FAILURE_FILENAME`: `check` = `final` / `before_row`, the error, the declared and executed blocks, the differing paths, the violations, the branches, the judged call counts and their digest, `ledger = {file, n_segments}`, the launch record, `FA_FAILURE_NOTE`) and re-raises; the throughput probe never writes one. `assert_admissible(run_dir)` refuses a marked directory by name ("NON-ADMISSIBLE (plan r2 amendment A6 (iv)) … restart the shard in a fresh out-dir once the cause is fixed"); it is the FIRST check of `read_fa_ledger` (every reopen: `run_pass`, the driver's preflight; and the merge census), of `_read_shard` (so the merge names it before its own run-summary requirement — a failed final pass writes no summary) and of `meshgrid_report.evaluate_run` (the per-arm report and every `meshgrid_crossarm` admission). **Disclosed out-of-scope edit:** that one line in `meshgrid_report.py` — the report reads no engine function per run directory that could carry the check, so this is the smallest change that puts the refusal in the report; nothing else in the report changed.
- **Tests** — a pass whose micro-batch is never filled: rows on disk, no run summary, the marker's schema and its counts re-deriving its executed block; a reopen (`run_pass` resume), `read_fa_ledger` and `assert_admissible` all refuse by name with the directory byte-unchanged; a before-row refusal (declared source chunk 16, run at 3) marks the shard too (`check: before_row`); a probe writes no marker; a shard that finished and whose later invocation's final check failed (simulated) is refused by the merge with nothing written; the per-arm report refuses a marked BF run, single-shard and merged.
- **Version Control** — commit `fc8836a7` (+53/−7 code lines, net +46; of these +1 in `meshgrid_report.py`).
- **Command / Validation** — RED 6 failed / 221 passed (`loc_meshgrid_2026-11-03_14:09:03_r2bf2_c6_non_admissible_red.log`) → GREEN fa_split 227 passed (the existing `…refused_before_any_row` test needed the marker writer to create the directory of a pass refused before its first write); report + crossarm + engine + launch-record + offgrid-probe 651 passed / 3 skipped.
- **Result** — passed.

## 2026-11-03T14:20:53-05:00 — rB-F2: THE AUTHORITATIVE GPU COMMAND LIST for R2-C (supersedes the command list of the rB-F1 closing entry; plan amendments A4–A6; Codex rB-F1 findings 4 and 6)
- **Supersedes** — the GPU command list of the entry "2026-11-03T00:27:56-05:00 — rB-F1 final verification and closing: Codex rB findings 1–8 addressed; the GPU command list for R2-C" (its pre-flight, gate, probe, shard / merge, resume, handoff, report, crossarm and restart bullets). That entry stays as written (this worklog is append-only); where the two differ, THIS list governs. Changes: (1) its "OPEN for the Planner" note on converting probe GPU-hours into a two-card makespan is CLOSED by the pre-registered A5 and A6 (ii) rules below; (2) every pre-flight check exits non-zero on failure (Codex rB-F1 NIT 6); (3) A6 (i), (iii) and (iv) are carried where they apply; (4) every gate's evidence is re-verified from its own JSON before it is committed (rB-F2 cycles 3–4); (5) a non-admissible shard and a ledger that does not verify are named (rB-F2 cycles 5–6). Every command line below is carried over verbatim except the two-lane probe lines (fully expanded here) and the handoff check (now exits non-zero).
- **When and where** — only after Yixun's go, on cards he released, never next to his processes (`readlink /proc/<pid>/cwd`); from a checkout of the round's REVIEWED, PUSHED pin that has `outputs_loc/`, `weights/` and `AcousticRooms/` (untracked in the FLAC checkout: symlink them into a worktree). Order (plan §3 R2-C 1, D-R2-2): every gate and the BF probe are pre-flight, before the first shard; then YAW on both cards, then BF; the BF shards additionally wait for the A5 verdict entry below.
- **Script head (NIT 6 — every check exits non-zero; written for a launch script under `set -euo pipefail`; in an interactive shell wrap each check in `( … )` so a STOP ends the subshell, not the terminal)** —
  - `set -euo pipefail; PY=/home/yixunhu/miniconda3/envs/flac/bin/python; PIN=<the reviewed, pushed 40-hex SHA>`
  - `test "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" = "$PIN" || { echo "STOP: HEAD $(git rev-parse HEAD) is not the reviewed pin $PIN"; exit 1; }`
  - `test -z "$(git status --porcelain --untracked-files=no)" || { echo "STOP: the tracked tree is dirty -- every gate would be non-PASS and every launch record refused"; exit 1; }`
  - tee each line to `worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/loc_meshgrid_<ts>_r2_<what>.log` (`… 2>&1 | tee <log>`; `pipefail` keeps the line's own exit code); commit every evidence JSON into the worklog folder.
- **Gates (unchanged lines; each exits 0 only on a FINAL PASS — comparison, autocast, identity, provenance — and the drift measurement only when complete)** —
  - **GV — YAW parity gate (minutes)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --cache-parity-check --expect-arm YAW --ckpt-path weights/exp20/YAW_40k.ckpt --cond-method vanilla --frame-avg-angles 0 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `vanilla_cache_parity.json`.
  - **G1 — split vs released, identical batching, autocast OFF (minutes)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --fa-split-parity-check --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast off --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `fa_split_parity.json`.
  - **G2 — memoization at matched batching + counter-tests (minutes)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --cache-parity-check --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `fa_cache_parity.json`.
  - **G3 — LOGIC GATE (A4 i: production partition vs faithful replay, fp32, hard 1e-3, all 16 registered probe queries anchored by the pinned `8c5fd5e8…`)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --fa-production-parity-check --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast off --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `fa_production_parity.json` (≈ 1.3 MB: per-chunk facts, rB-F2 cycle 2). Cost not measured (every probe query's full candidate set generated twice); no smoke form (A4 v).
  - **G3D — DRIFT MEASUREMENT (A4 ii; A6 iii)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --fa-production-drift-measurement --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `fa_production_drift.json` — `verdict: "measurement"` with `tolerance`, `score_tolerance` and `match` all null; published beside the 3.9e-3 vanilla batching caveat; no fp16 tolerance is chosen before or after.
  - **G0 — vanilla no-regression (A6000 class only; ≈ 10 min + stream walk)**: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --replay-check --replay-against outputs_loc/exp22/i1_P1_CRN_br256_20260825_194053_merged --ckpt-path weights/exp20/P1_40k.ckpt --cond-method vanilla --frame-avg-angles 0 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --dataset-root AcousticRooms --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates` → `vanilla_replay_regression.json` (the anchor is the committed `offgrid_probe_report.json`, `8c5fd5e8…`, by default; the evidence now carries each query's frozen digests and the published binding).
  - **A6 (i)** — each gate's `inputs.binding.cond_autocast` keeps the registered token (`default`, or `off` for G1 / G3: the binding digest depends on it) and its evidence ALSO carries the resolved `cond_autocast_requested`, `cond_autocast_enabled`, `cond_autocast_dtype` and `cond_autocast_device_type` (published by every gate; for G1, G3 and G3D the requested / enabled / dtype triple is also a verdict clause — another or a missing state is not a PASS, or not a complete measurement); `default` is never the sole description of how a gate ran.
  - **Re-verify every evidence from its JSON before committing it (rB-F2)** — for `<name>` in `vanilla_cache_parity`, `fa_split_parity`, `fa_cache_parity`, `fa_production_parity`, `fa_production_drift`, `vanilla_replay_regression`: `$PY -c "import json, sys; from src.localization import meshgrid_fa_parity as fp; r = fp.reverify_gate_evidence(json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))); print('RE-VERIFIED' if not r else chr(10).join(r)); sys.exit(1 if r else 0)" outputs_loc/exp22/r2_gates/<name>.json || { echo "STOP: <name>.json does not re-verify from its JSON"; exit 1; }`. `[]` → `RE-VERIFIED`; anything else names the clause that does not hold, and the evidence is not committed as a PASS.
- **BF throughput probe (no-quality) and the pre-registered makespan rule** —
  - one card: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=<free card> $PY localize_meshgrid.py --probe 1 --probe-room Bathrooms/Bathrooms_idx_14 --probe-stem BF_bathrooms14 --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_probe_BF` → `diagnostics_BF_bathrooms14.json`.
  - OR the concurrent two-lane form (no card restriction; both lines started together and both awaited): `$PY localize_meshgrid.py --probe 1 --probe-room Bathrooms/Bathrooms_idx_14 --probe-stem BF_bathrooms14_lane0 --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:0 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_probe_BF_lane0 & L0=$!; $PY localize_meshgrid.py --probe 1 --probe-room Bathrooms/Bathrooms_idx_14 --probe-stem BF_bathrooms14_lane1 --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --cond-method fa_invariant --frame-avg-angles 0 90 180 270 --rotate-deg 0 --cond-autocast default --batch-rows 256 --source-chunk 16 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --device cuda:1 --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_probe_BF_lane1 & L1=$!; S0=0; wait $L0 || S0=$?; S1=0; wait $L1 || S1=$?; test "$S0$S1" = 00 || { echo "STOP: probe lane exit codes $S0 / $S1"; exit 1; }` → `diagnostics_BF_bathrooms14_lane0.json`, `diagnostics_BF_bathrooms14_lane1.json`.
  - **A5 (pre-registered 2026-11-03 00:30 EST)** — the probe projects total GPU-hours for the whole 5,337-query pass (`projection.projected_gpu_hours.total`); the projected two-card MAKESPAN compared with the **150 h** cap (D-R2-4) is **`makespan_proj = contention_factor × GPU_hours_proj × 0.5145`** (0.5145 = 74.5 h / 144.8 GPU-h, P1's measured longest-shard share under the same fixed cafe / rest15 split; the cafe shard is the critical path), with **`contention_factor = 1.5` for a ONE-card probe** (A4 iv: the released fa path rotates the 3×256×512 float64 panorama on the CPU per candidate and angle).
  - **A6 (ii)** — for the concurrent TWO-lane probe (`contention_factor = 1.0`) each lane projects total GPU-hours from its own throughput measured under the shared contention, and the SLOWER lane counts: **`makespan_proj = 1.0 × max(GPU_hours_proj_lane0, GPU_hours_proj_lane1) × 0.5145`**.
  - the arithmetic, one card (exits non-zero above the cap): `$PY -c "import json; h = json.load(open('outputs_loc/exp22/r2_probe_BF/diagnostics_BF_bathrooms14.json'))['projection']['projected_gpu_hours']['total']; m = 1.5 * h * 0.5145; print(f'A5 one-card probe: makespan_proj = 1.5 x {h:.1f} GPU-h x 0.5145 = {m:.1f} h vs the 150 h cap -> ' + ('WITHIN THE CAP' if m <= 150 else 'ABOVE THE CAP: STOP AND ASK YIXUN, NO SHARD STARTS')); raise SystemExit(0 if m <= 150 else 1)"`.
  - the arithmetic, two lanes (exits non-zero above the cap): `$PY -c "import json; h = [json.load(open(f'outputs_loc/exp22/r2_probe_BF_lane{i}/diagnostics_BF_bathrooms14_lane{i}.json'))['projection']['projected_gpu_hours']['total'] for i in (0, 1)]; m = 1.0 * max(h) * 0.5145; print(f'A6 (ii) two-lane probe: makespan_proj = 1.0 x max({h[0]:.1f}, {h[1]:.1f}) GPU-h x 0.5145 = {m:.1f} h vs the 150 h cap -> ' + ('WITHIN THE CAP' if m <= 150 else 'ABOVE THE CAP: STOP AND ASK YIXUN, NO SHARD STARTS')); raise SystemExit(0 if m <= 150 else 1)"`.
  - **Before any BF shard starts** — the probe evidence (the diagnostics JSON, committed into the worklog folder), this arithmetic (its printed line, with the probe form and the factor used) and the verdict against 150 h are written into this worklog as a dated entry. A projection ABOVE 150 h stops and asks Yixun: no BF shard starts.

  - BF merge (CPU; re-derives both shards' executed partition through the validating ledger reader): `$PY localize_meshgrid.py --merge-shards outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_${TAG}_cafe outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_${TAG}_rest15 --merge-out outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_${TAG}_merged --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json`
  - **A6 (iv) — a non-admissible shard** — a shard whose executed-partition check fails (the final exact check, or the check before a row) writes `partition_check_failed.json`; its rows are INCOMPLETE ARTIFACTS: `--merge-shards`, the per-arm report and the crossarm refuse it by name ("NON-ADMISSIBLE"), and so does every reopen (`--resume`, or a relaunch into the same out-dir). Fix the cause, then RESTART the shard in a fresh out-dir with fresh launch records — never resume it. Before each merge, name it first: `for d in outputs_loc/exp22/r2_<ARM>_${TAG}_cafe outputs_loc/exp22/r2_<ARM>_${TAG}_rest15; do test ! -e "$d/partition_check_failed.json" || { echo "STOP: $d is NON-ADMISSIBLE (A6 iv) -- restart it in a fresh out-dir"; exit 1; }; done`.
  - **Ledger integrity (rB-F2 cycle 5)** — a BF shard's `fa_partition_ledger.json` is read through ONE validating reader at every reopen (the driver refuses in preflight — before the engine is built or anything is written — with `REFUSED: out-dir …: … does not verify: …`) and at the merge (nothing written). Such a refusal means the shard's executed-partition evidence no longer verifies: restart the shard; never edit a ledger or a run summary.
- **Crash at the SAME pin (D-R2-6)** — emit a NEW record for the identical command plus `--resume` (e.g. `…/launch_record_resume1.json`), then run it with `--launch-record …/launch_record_resume1.json … --resume`: a new launch segment and a new executed-partition ledger segment; merge as above. Refused (restart instead) when the shard carries `partition_check_failed.json` (A6 iv) or its ledger does not verify.
- **Handoff preflight (A3; before any report / crossarm line; NIT 6 — exits non-zero)** — `test "$($PY -c 'from src.localization import meshgrid_report as mr; print(mr.REGISTERED_RETRIEVAL_HANDOFF_SHA256)')" = "$(sha256sum worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json | cut -d' ' -f1)" || { echo "STOP: HANDOFF MISMATCH -- the retrieval handoff is not the pinned 1bf62c05..."; exit 1; } && echo HANDOFF OK` (the committed handoff hashes to `1bf62c05…`).
- **Per-arm reports (A3)** — YAW: `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_report --run-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_YAW_${TAG}_merged --ckpt-path weights/exp20/YAW_40k.ckpt --expect-ckpt-sha256 ac1f26034e4f341fe0c2cb4638e2eb473959d66ddd2fd95d184dc2fd4f264de7 --expect-arm YAW --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_report_YAW`; BF: `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_report --run-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_${TAG}_merged --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --expect-ckpt-sha256 5319feb4af874624859e87105ddd8ab06d4b449769d1e054f712b2b1c0542328 --expect-arm BF --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_report_BF`. Each refuses a run directory carrying `partition_check_failed.json` by name (A6 iv).
- **Crossarm (A1 + A3)** — `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_crossarm --run-p1 outputs_loc/exp22/i1_P1_CRN_br256_20260825_194053_merged --run-yaw outputs_loc/exp22/r2_YAW_<YAW TAG>_merged --run-bf outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_<BF TAG>_merged --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --allow-legacy-p1-provenance --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_crossarm`.
- **Restart, not resume (A3 ii; A6 iv)** — any code change after a shard has started invalidates `--resume` for that run's pin: restart the affected shard at the new pin (fresh out-dir, fresh records); mixed git SHAs in one run are refused at the merge (A2). ## 2026-11-03T16:11:38-05:00 — rB-F3 cycle 4: a missing ledger is admitted only in a FRESH out-dir; real-int segment indices; the ledger is on disk before the binding (Codex rB-F2 finding 3, HIGH, part 1)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F2 3: "`read_fa_ledger` returns `None` when both ledger and summary block are absent ..., even if an existing binding and rows show that this is not a fresh directory"; "Segment indices also accept `False`/`True` as `0`/`1`"; the brief: "Refuse a missing fa ledger (and a missing summary block) whenever the out-dir already holds a binding, any row, a launch-record segment or any other run artifact — only a genuinely fresh out-dir may start a ledger".
- **Change** — `fresh_out_dir(out_dir)`: fresh = absent, or holding nothing but top-level `*.json` files that no pass writes (the operator's emitted launch records: the registered lines emit `<shard>/launch_record.json` before the shard's first run); anything else — the binding, the run summary, the merge report, the ledger or its failure marker, `launch_segments.json`, any directory (`rows/`, `launch_records/`), a `.tmp` half-write, a `diagnostics_*.json` probe record, any non-JSON file — makes it non-fresh. `read_fa_ledger` refuses a missing ledger in a non-fresh out-dir by name (with the summary present or deleted), and at a merge always. A ledger on which nothing has run yet is a consistent prefix at a reopen (the partition check runs at a reopen only once a call is recorded; always at a merge). Segment indices must be real ints. Ordering, so the rule never refuses a first invocation: `open_fa_ledger` publishes the new segment at once; the driver's `claim_out_dir` now opens it FIRST (after the read-only `assert_resumable` / `assert_binding` checks, before `write_binding`, an advisory record and the launch segment) and returns `(done, segment, ledger)`; `run_pass(fa_ledger=…)` adopts it only if it is the ledger just opened for that out-dir, declaration and launch record (empty segment) and otherwise opens its own (direct engine use on a fresh directory); a vanilla claim opens none and writes in its old order. The noise-policy refusal moved ahead of the ledger open.
- **Reading (disclosed)** — "Refuse a missing fa ledger (and a missing summary block) whenever …": read as "a missing ledger — with or without its summary block — is refused in a non-fresh out-dir". A missing SUMMARY with a ledger present stays admitted at a reopen (a first invocation that crashed has none); the row binding of cycle 6 binds such a ledger to the published rows.
- **Fixtures (disclosed)** — follow the driver's order (ledger, binding, launch segment): `_claim_fa` for the fa_split shards and the merge test's "only invocation" shard, the report's synthetic BF shard opens its ledger before its binding; the launch-record claim test unpacks the triple.

## 2026-11-03T16:18:09-05:00 — rB-F3 cycle 5: the merged BF ledger is valid under its own reader, and the per-arm report re-reads it and joins it to the merge receipt (Codex rB-F2 finding 1, BLOCKING)

Two one-segment shards therefore produce indices `[0, 0]`, which `_ledger_reasons` correctly rejects"; "`evaluate_run` checks only the failure marker ...; `assert_merge_report` ignores both the merged ledger and `merge_report.json.fa_partition_executed`"; "Vanilla reporting must conversely require no FA ledger or receipt."
- **Change** — (a) `fa_partition_census` renumbers the merged segments globally `0..n−1` (shard order as given, then each shard's own order), each keeping `shard` (the shard dir as given) and `shard_segment_index` (its shard-local index), and returns `{ledger, receipt}` only after verifying both exactly as the reader verifies a merged run — so the merge writes nothing it would itself refuse; `merge_shards` writes them unchanged (receipt keys as before: executed, violations, counts_sha256, per_shard). A vanilla run (`declared` None) may carry no ledger, no run-summary block and no NON-NULL receipt; absent (the Aug-2026 P1 merged run has no such key — checked on the real directory, read-only) and null (what every YAW merge writes) are admitted. (c) `meshgrid_report.evaluate_run` calls `me.read_fa_ledger(run_dir, binding["fa_partition"], exact=True)` right after the binding is recomputed — a merged BF run is joined to its receipt, a single-shard BF run to its run summary, a vanilla run must carry neither; `meshgrid_crossarm.admit_run` inherits the refusal through `evaluate_run`. ## 2026-11-03T16:46:38-05:00 — rB-F3 cycle 6: every ledger state is bound to the published rows (Codex rB-F2 finding 3, HIGH, part 2)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F2 3: "A truncated tail segment with recomputed self-digests and no summary can pass `final=False`; later full-sized calls can saturate the maxima and make the final exact partition pass despite the lost call counts ... bind each segment state to its published rows. A practical design is to stamp each row with that segment's cumulative `n_queries` and counts digest, allowing only a single one-query-ahead state for an interrupted final write"; the brief: "Codex's design is the default ... If you choose a different but equivalent binding, justify it against Codex's probe in the worklog."
- **Binding chosen, and why it is not literally Codex's in-row stamp (disclosed)** — the first GREEN attempt put the stamp IN the row (`row["fa_ledger_state"]`): 9 crossarm tests then failed, because `meshgrid_crossarm.pair_runs` refuses every row field outside its `PAIRING_WHITELIST` (BF rows would differ from P1 / YAW rows on `row.fa_ledger_state`), and that whitelist lives in the PROTECTED `meshgrid_crossarm.py`. Hiding the field from the crossarm in the report would have circumvented the protected gate. So the stamp lives BESIDE the row — `<out-dir>/fa_ledger_stamps/<room>/q<position>.json = {segment_index, n_queries, counts_sha256, query_id}` — written atomically after the ledger write and before the row write, copied by the merge with its row; a BF row keeps exactly the vanilla row schema (tested), vanilla passes write no stamp. **Equivalence against Codex's probe**: a row's own `row_sha256` is a self-digest anyone can recompute, so Codex's in-row stamp is defeated only by rewriting the ledger AND re-signing the last row; the sidecar is defeated only by rewriting the ledger AND the last row's stamp file — the same two-file coordinated edit. Both refuse the probe (truncated tail, recomputed self-digests, summary deleted) at the reopen, before a resume writes anything. The one-line alternative (whitelisting `row.fa_ledger_state` in `meshgrid_crossarm.PAIRING_WHITELIST`, beside `launch_record_sha256`) is the Planner's to choose; it was NOT made.
- **The one-ahead state, and the merge (reading disclosed)** — each segment keeps `delta_counts` (the calls since its state at `n_queries − 1`; canonical, inside its counts). With N = the segment's `n_queries` and M = the highest `n_queries` its self-verifying rows' stamps carry: N == M → its counts digest must be the last row's stamped state (the empty state when M = 0); N == M + 1 → ONE query ahead (killed between the ledger write and the row write) → `counts − delta_counts` must be the last row's stamped state, so the prefix every published row rests on is bound and only the lost attempt's calls are not; anything else (behind, or two ahead) is refused. "Admitting ONLY a single one-query-ahead state at reopen … and exact equality at merge" is read PER SEGMENT and for the run's LAST state: at a reopen any segment may be one ahead; at a merge (`exact`) a shard's LAST invocation must be exact (it completed and wrote the run summary), while an earlier crashed segment stays one ahead forever (its lost row is regenerated by the resume, in a later segment) and is admitted only if every call shape of its lost attempt was executed again by a later segment of the same shard (the resume re-ran that query at the same declared batching) — so a one-ahead state can neither HIDE a call (its prefix is bound) nor INVENT a call shape (e.g. a phantom full-size call that would saturate a maximum). Literal "exact equality at merge" for every segment would refuse every crash-and-resume shard, which D-R2-6 admits. Intermediate stamps (n < M) are not bound to stored states (the ledger keeps the final state only, as in Codex's design); a row whose own digest fails is ignored here (a resume regenerates it, the merge refuses it); regenerating the LAST row of an earlier segment leaves that segment one ahead (admitted, bound), regenerating its last two leaves it two ahead (refused: restart).
- **Change** — `persist_fa_ledger` writes `delta_counts` (and refuses a recorder that lost calls); `write_fa_stamp` / `read_fa_stamp` / `fa_stamp_path` (`FA_STAMPS_DIRNAME`); `_row_binding_reasons` + `_segment_binding` (the rule above; R1: every self-verifying row's stamp is a real `{segment_index, n_queries, counts_sha256, query_id}` state of a held segment — in merged mode mapped by the row's launch record and the shard-local index —, naming its own query, never claimed twice nor beyond its segment); `_stamp_reasons` runs it from `read_fa_ledger` (reopen, merge census, merged and single-shard report) after the summary / receipt join; `merge_shards` binds the merged ledger to the rows it is about to publish, in memory, before any write, and copies each stamp; `FA_LEDGER_NOTE` describes the delta and the stamps.
- **Fixture (disclosed)** — the report's synthetic BF shard persists one ledger state per row and writes its stamp (all synthetic calls fall on the first state).
- **Tests** — every fa row has the stamp of the state persisted before it, the last equal to the segment's state, counts − delta hashing to the previous row's stamp, and the row schema equal to a vanilla row's; Codex's probe in four forms (full-size or tail-size rows dropped × delta emptied or one-ahead claimed) refused by the reader and at reopen with the directory byte-unchanged; a real kill between the ledger and the row writes is admitted one ahead, and refused when its delta is emptied, its count moved past the rows, or behind them; after the resume the shard merges, and a phantom lost-attempt shape or a one-ahead LAST invocation is refused; a moved / mis-named / incomplete / deleted stamp of the last row and a state claimed by two rows are refused; a merged report refuses a moved or deleted merged stamp.
- **Version Control** — commit `3b410489` (+130/−8 code lines, net +122).
- **Command / Validation** — RED 13 failed (`loc_meshgrid_2026-11-03_16:20:56_r2bf3_c6_row_binding_red.log`: all four probe forms ADMITTED by the reader at HEAD — "DID NOT RAISE"; the one-ahead edits admitted) → GREEN (stamp beside the row) the ten focused files 1322 passed / 9 skipped.
- **Result** — passed.

## 2026-11-03T16:52:24-05:00 — rB-F3 cycle 7: the kill window is closed (Codex rB-F2 NIT 5; plan r2 amendment A6 iv)
- **Goal** — Codex rB-F2 5: "if the process is killed after the check fails and before `write_partition_failure` completes, the before-row ledger still holds the previous good prefix, while the final ledger can be accepted under the prefix rule ... atomically publish a pending-check sentinel before each check and clear it only after success. Probes write none (no ledger). **Cost**: per query one small atomic JSON write (tmp + rename) and one unlink, next to minutes of GPU work per query — negligible; the window a kill must land in to force a restart is the CPU-only check itself (microseconds of a minutes-long cycle).

  - **A6 (iv) — a non-admissible shard** — a shard whose executed-partition check fails (the final exact check, or the check before a row) writes `partition_check_failed.json`; its rows are INCOMPLETE ARTIFACTS: `--merge-shards`, the per-arm report and the crossarm refuse it by name ("NON-ADMISSIBLE"), and so does every reopen (`--resume`, or a relaunch into the same out-dir). Fix the cause, then RESTART the shard in a fresh out-dir with fresh launch records — never resume it. A shard killed WHILE an executed-partition check ran still carries `partition_check_pending.json` (rB-F3; Codex rB-F2 NIT 5) and is non-admissible in the same way: refused by name by the merge, the per-arm report, the crossarm and every reopen; restart it. Before each merge, name both first: `for d in outputs_loc/exp22/r2_<ARM>_${TAG}_cafe outputs_loc/exp22/r2_<ARM>_${TAG}_rest15; do for f in partition_check_failed.json partition_check_pending.json; do test ! -e "$d/$f" || { echo "STOP: $d carries $f -- NON-ADMISSIBLE (A6 iv): restart it in a fresh out-dir"; exit 1; }; done; done`.
  - **Ledger integrity (rB-F2 cycle 5)** — a BF shard's `fa_partition_ledger.json` is read through ONE validating reader at every reopen (the driver refuses in preflight — before the engine is built or anything is written — with `REFUSED: out-dir …: … does not verify: …`) and at the merge (nothing written). Such a refusal means the shard's executed-partition evidence no longer verifies: restart the shard; never edit a ledger or a run summary. (rB-F3) The reader also refuses: a missing ledger in an out-dir that is not FRESH (anything but the emitted launch records in it — the ledger is opened and written FIRST, before the binding, by the driver itself); a row whose ledger stamp (`fa_ledger_stamps/<room>/q<position>.json`, written just before the row) does not bind its segment's state; and, for the MERGED directory, a ledger or a `merge_report.json` receipt that do not join — the BF per-arm report re-reads the merged ledger exactly. Never edit a ledger, a stamp, a run summary or a merge report.
- **Crash at the SAME pin (D-R2-6)** — emit a NEW record for the identical command plus `--resume` (e.g. `…/launch_record_resume1.json`), then run it with `--launch-record …/launch_record_resume1.json … --resume`: a new launch segment and a new executed-partition ledger segment; merge as above. Refused (restart instead) when the shard carries `partition_check_failed.json` or `partition_check_pending.json` (A6 iv), when its ledger is missing (rB-F3: a shard whose ledger was lost is never re-ledgered), or when its ledger or a row stamp does not verify. A resume after a kill between the ledger write and the row write is admitted: the ledger is then exactly one query ahead of its rows, bound by its delta, and the merge admits that state only if the resume re-ran the lost query.
- **Handoff preflight (A3; before any report / crossarm line; NIT 6 — exits non-zero)** — `test "$($PY -c 'from src.localization import meshgrid_report as mr; print(mr.REGISTERED_RETRIEVAL_HANDOFF_SHA256)')" = "$(sha256sum worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json | cut -d' ' -f1)" || { echo "STOP: HANDOFF MISMATCH -- the retrieval handoff is not the pinned 1bf62c05..."; exit 1; } && echo HANDOFF OK` (the committed handoff hashes to `1bf62c05…`).
- **Per-arm reports (A3)** — YAW: `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_report --run-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_YAW_${TAG}_merged --ckpt-path weights/exp20/YAW_40k.ckpt --expect-ckpt-sha256 ac1f26034e4f341fe0c2cb4638e2eb473959d66ddd2fd95d184dc2fd4f264de7 --expect-arm YAW --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_report_YAW`; BF: `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_report --run-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_${TAG}_merged --ckpt-path weights/exp20/BF_40k.ckpt --expect-ckpt-sha256 5319feb4af874624859e87105ddd8ab06d4b449769d1e054f712b2b1c0542328 --expect-arm BF --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_report_BF`. Each refuses a run directory carrying `partition_check_failed.json` or `partition_check_pending.json` by name (A6 iv); the BF report also re-reads the merged executed-partition ledger exactly and joins it to `merge_report.json`'s receipt (and its rows' stamps), and the YAW report refuses an fa ledger or a non-null receipt (rB-F3).
- **Crossarm (A1 + A3)** — `$PY -m src.localization.meshgrid_crossarm --run-p1 outputs_loc/exp22/i1_P1_CRN_br256_20260825_194053_merged --run-yaw outputs_loc/exp22/r2_YAW_<YAW TAG>_merged --run-bf outputs_loc/exp22/r2_BF_<BF TAG>_merged --context-manifest outputs_loc/exp22/d1_context_manifest.json --audit-report outputs_loc/exp22/g1_audit/geometry_audit_report.json --metadata-root AcousticRooms/metadata --expect-metadata-bank-sha256 9f1322e538dccbf36c330f3a629c1ad37913627db7b93290c0c14cc9bf47fe74 --allow-legacy-p1-provenance --retrieval-handoff worklog/worklog_yixun/exp_22_loc_meshgrid_claude/retrieval_control_handoff.json --out-dir outputs_loc/exp22/r2_crossarm`.
- **Restart, not resume (A3 ii; A6 iv)** — any code change after a shard has started invalidates `--resume` for that run's pin: restart the affected shard at the new pin (fresh out-dir, fresh records); mixed git SHAs in one run are refused at the merge (A2). A non-admissible shard is likewise restarted, never resumed.

- **Schemas** — row stamp `fa_ledger_stamps/<room_stem>/q<position:05d>.json = {segment_index, n_queries, counts_sha256, query_id}` (fa only; the row itself keeps the vanilla schema); ledger segment `{segment_index, n_queries, launch_record_sha256, counts, counts_sha256, delta_counts}`; merged ledger segment = the shard segment with `segment_index` global `0..n−1` and `shard` + `shard_segment_index` added; merged ledger `{declared, segments, note, merged}` joined to `merge_report.json.fa_partition_executed = {executed, violations, counts_sha256, per_shard: [{dir, counts_sha256, n_segments}]}`; sentinel `partition_check_pending.json = {note, check, n_segments, created_utc}`.
- **Ambiguities resolved (each a reading, disclosed)** — (a) "stamp each fa row with that segment's cumulative `n_queries` and counts digest": the stamp lives BESIDE the row (cycle 6) because `meshgrid_crossarm.PAIRING_WHITELIST` (protected) refuses any new row field; equivalent against Codex's probe (both need a coordinated ledger + one-file edit); the in-row form needs a one-line whitelist entry in the protected module — the Planner's choice, not made. (b) "admitting ONLY a single one-query-ahead state at reopen … and exact equality at merge": per segment against its own last row; at a merge the LAST invocation of each shard must be exact, an earlier crashed segment stays one ahead (bound by its prefix) and is admitted only if every call shape of its lost attempt was executed again later — a literal "every segment exact at merge" would refuse every crash-and-resume shard (D-R2-6). (c) "Refuse a missing fa ledger (and a missing summary block) whenever …": a missing ledger, with or without its summary, in a non-fresh out-dir; a missing SUMMARY beside a ledger stays admitted at a reopen. (d) "only a genuinely fresh out-dir": absent, or holding only top-level `*.json` files no pass writes (the registered lines emit `<shard>/launch_record.json` before the first run); to keep a first invocation admissible, the driver opens (and writes) the ledger BEFORE the binding and `run_pass` adopts it. (e) "the merged ledger written by the merge must pass `read_fa_ledger(merged_dir, declared, exact=True)`": a directory publishing `merge_report.json` is read in MERGED mode — joined to the receipt, never reopened (no run summary is written into a merged directory). (f) "a non-null fa receipt block, is a named refusal; an absent one is the only admitted state": absent OR null is admitted (every YAW merge writes null; the Aug-2026 P1 run has no key). (g) "Autocast clause: check the EXACT resolved state against `inputs.device`": the registered A4 clause of G1 / G3 / G3D; G2 / G0 / GV keep publishing the state without a value clause, as before (type-checked by the schema; their token is pinned by identity) — so the 14:20:53 description "for G1, G3 and G3D … also a verdict clause" still holds. (h) "`inputs_reasons` requires the exact registered DiT-input key set": enforced in the LIVE comparison too, so a live PASS always re-verifies. (i) "every per-key delta and every score delta … a finite number": finite AND non-negative (a |diff| is never negative), the two agreement flags booleans, live and re-verified. (j) The sweep "→ a non-empty reason list" needed the full schema: exact key sets, JSON types (a bool is never an int, an int never a float), the registered note / reverify_note. (k) A zero-count counts row is refused as non-canonical (a call shape no recorder observed would still move the executed block's maxima). (l) "remove it only after the check passed": a failed check leaves the sentinel beside the failure marker (both refuse; the marker is named first). (m) Mutation coverage "every new clause … AND the existing re-verifier paths": where an rB-F2 tamper was also caught by a second clause, an isolating tamper was added first, so each of the 70 deletions is observable on its own.
- **Not covered, and why** — a coordinated rewrite of the ledger AND the last row's stamp (or AND the merge receipt) re-verifies by construction: every artifact is self-digested by public functions, the same bar as Codex's in-row design; intermediate stamps (n below a segment's last row) are not bound to stored states (the ledger keeps the final state and the delta only); a one-ahead state's lost-attempt call COUNTS are unbound (its prefix is bound, its SHAPES are bound at a merge; counts do not enter the executed block); G1's `executed_partition` is type-checked but not re-derived (G1's verdict does not depend on it); G2 / G0 / GV autocast values are published, not judged (unchanged); everything was exercised on CPU fixtures only — no real-stack or GPU run.
